# Blackwood Horror — blind round 2

Frozen before source inspection. Played solely through currently offered `llm.lua --links` numbered actions. Every accepted action resumed/saved `/tmp/blackwood-r2.sav`; the save contents were never read. Started with `/tmp/lua5.4 llm.lua --new-game --links --game blackwood-horror --save /tmp/blackwood-r2.sav`. The exact subsequent CLI commands and complete JSON responses follow below. A command wrapper matched each requested command against the current offered actions, then selected its 1-based index. Later it refreshed `--links` immediately before selecting because shared runtime edits changed menu indices mid-session. Rejected selections are retained as tooling evidence.

## Ending status

NOT COMPLETED. Reached garden chapel gate after exploring reception, operating theater, storage/boiler, flooded chamber, isolation, electroshock/padded cell, observation, director office, staff quarters and cafeteria. Found/read the 1947 and Patient 189 clues. Opened reception drawer and director safe. Saw chapel key but could not take it after a shared runtime change introduced unsaved PLAY_RANDOM state: take crashed with `link_play.lua:37: attempt to perform arithmetic on a nil value (field 'PLAY_RANDOM')`. Parent requested freeze here because old saves intentionally receive no compatibility migration. This is a tooling interruption, not a claim that the book cannot be completed.

## Findings observed blind

- **Boiler puzzle blocker:** took/lit oil lantern, took shovel, took coal (shovel-specific success), put coal in boiler. `burn boiler with lanter` said `With a oil lantern??!?`; `turn on boiler with lanter` said `The oil lantern is already lit.`. `turn on boiler` said it needed fuel and flame. Unable to generate steam; sealed flooded-chamber door says steam may loosen it. Instrument-side lantern handler appears to steal the boiler action, while generic burn syntax refuses lantern as instrument.
- **Nested key possession blocker with organic workaround:** open red book reveals safe key. Menu says `drop safe key`, but `open safe with safe key` says `The safe is locked. You need the key.`. Dropped safe key, dropped logbook to free inventory slot, took safe key, then same open command succeeded. A key inside a carried book should count as held or be explicitly taken by the action.
- **Bottle linked target invalid:** open theater cabinet introduces bottle using target `ether bottle`. Both offered `examine ether bottle` and `take ether bottle` said `That sentence isn't one I recognize.`. Other bottle verbs use same target.
- **Nested visible vial scope:** opening bag on storage shelves exposes offered `examine vial`; response `You can't see any vial here!`. Taking bag then examining vial succeeds. Child visibility differs with parent depth.
- **Record ambiguity:** `read records` in storage while carrying patient file asks medical records vs patient file. Offered `read record` asks the same, so menus initially do not resolve the ambiguity. Runtime later switched to full adjective/noun phrases; not independently retested here.
- **Affordance gaps:** wheel valve offered only `turn valve with ...`, no plain turn. Electroshock machine offered odd two-object turn-to/for actions; did not exercise it. Runtime's syntax rules need inspect after freeze.
- **Menu quality:** initially hundreds of nonsensical read/open-with actions; duplicate targets such as `lanter` and `lantern`, `safe` and `wall safe`; awkward labels `Go west west`. Parent changed shared runtime during play to reduce these and use canonical names. Later menus looked substantially cleaner.
- **State/text polish:** lantern examination says its steady flame can kindle larger fire even before lighting (minor state-inconsistent wording). Padded cell repeats east doorway in room and door item description. Staff lockers generic listing says `The lockers contains:`. A mass of auto-taken records caused inventory cap during safe key workaround; portable clue documents need coherent intentional behavior.
- **Good behavior:** gate/entrance doors give clear immovable responses; reception key 3→drawer straightforward; diary clues readable and integrated; crayon drawing stays pinned with bespoke refusal; flashlight explicitly dead rather than interchangeable lantern; coal/shovel usage distinct; revisits print actual room prose.

Bookshelves line the walls, filled with medical texts and journals.\u000aThe observation logbook lies here.\u000aA massive desk dominates the room.\u000aThe portrait of Dr. Mordecai hangs swung aside on its hinge, his stern eyes still following you.\u000aWhere the portrait has swung aside, a wall safe is set into the paneling.\u000aPrivate notes in a tight, obsessive hand detail experiments that should never have been conducted.\u000aA heavy iron key lies among the safe's contents, its head carved with a cross.\u000aTo the west, a door opens back to the administrative wing
